# New Harness — working memory (canonical project home)

> **🧯 THE "STUCK TURN" WEDGE — ROOT-CAUSED + FIXED (2026-08-14, Opus-5 builder; Mac-verify PENDING, ⚠️ PENDING FABLE QA):** Debi's Hermes-lane turn on the local 4B hung in prefill (`thinking…` + the bridge's 20s *"hermes is working — large prompt prefill…"* note), **Stop appeared not to work, `＋ New` did nothing at all, and the AGENT/CHAT chips would not detach** — the panel needed an app restart. **ROOT CAUSE: the panel did not OWN the end of its own turn.** `chatBusy` was cleared in exactly ONE place — `sendChat`'s `finally` — which only runs when `reader.read()` returns or throws. A relay that neither answers nor closes therefore pinned `chatBusy` TRUE **forever**, and every escape hatch was written as a silent early return: `newSession()` `if (chatBusy) return;` (that IS the dead ＋NEW), `selectSession()` / `selectHermesSession()` / `duplicateSession()` the same, and — the sharpest one — **`setMode()` flipped `chatMode` to `'agent'`/`'chat'` while the Hermes stream was still running, after which `sendChat`'s busy branch (`if (chatMode === 'hermes') hermesStop()`) no longer offered Stop AT ALL: the button still READ "Stop" and did nothing.** That is the dead end. `hermesStop()` itself only fired the interrupt RPC and then *hoped* — its own comment said "the stream teardown restores the button" — and it early-returned on `!hermesSid`, so in the **pre-first-event phase Stop was a guaranteed no-op**. The bridge's watchdogs were all working as designed and none of them could help: the 60s first-event guard only fires when `not got_any` (Debi's turn HAD events), the `active_list` probe **returns True on any doubt** (correct — a probe misread must never kill a live stream) and the model genuinely WAS "working", so the only remaining bound was the **600s hard guard** — ten minutes, i.e. "stuck forever" from a human's seat. **FIX — the panel now owns the turn.** Every turn gets `curTurn = {lane, stage, ctl: AbortController, holder, ended, done, timer}`; `forceEndTurn(reason)` aborts the fetch (**which is what unblocks the reader, so the EXISTING `finally` does the real cleanup**), stamps `interrupted (forced) · <stage>` on the existing `.statusline`, and schedules `turnHardRelease()` 400ms later as belt-and-braces in case even the abort is ignored. `stopTurnNow(reason)` is the ONE path out, reached by **Stop, ＋NEW, a lane switch and a session click** — it fires the gateway interrupt **without awaiting it** (`session.interrupt` can itself stall for its own 10s RPC timeout) and force-ends locally regardless. `hermesStop()` now arms the 3s client force-end **before** the `!hermesSid` guard, so Stop wins in the pre-first-event phase too; and the busy branch routes on **`curTurn.lane`**, not the current chip, so a lane switch can never make Stop unreachable. **＋NEW and the lane chips STOP the turn and proceed** (Fable's preferred shape), with an activity-feed line naming the stage it died in. **PANEL WATCHDOG + BRIDGE HEARTBEAT:** the relay now yields a tiny `{"type":"hermes_ping"}` on **every** waiting tick — including while an approval card is pending, which can legitimately wait minutes — and the panel arms `TURN_FIRSTBYTE_MS = 90000` at send and re-arms `TURN_STALL_MS = 75000` on **every received chunk**, so "the model is slow" is now distinguishable from "the relay is gone" without waiting out the 600s guard. The ping is `continue`d before the inspect log so it cannot flood it. ⚠️ **the watchdog is HERMES-LANE ONLY** — that lane has the heartbeat; the direct/agent lanes emit nothing during a long local prefill and arming there could kill a healthy turn (they get the escape hatches, which is the actual fix for deadness). **DIAGNOSTIC:** each turn labels its stage (`connecting` → `waiting for the first frame` → `prefill (bridge says hermes is working)` → `thinking`/`answering`/`running a tool`/`awaiting approval` → `done`) and a force-end prints it to the console AND the feed — the absence of exactly this is why the report could not be diagnosed from the outside. **NOT CAUSED BY A RECENT SLICE** — conv-mode's `turn_start`/`turn_end` hooks, auto-grow and split-view all sit inside the same try/finally and were audited clean; the `if (chatBusy) return` guards have been there since the Phase-3 rail fix, and Hermes is still at **v2026.7.30** (the v2026.8.13 bump is a pending runbook step and is NOT implicated). **A completed turn can never be force-ended** (`turn.done` is set at `[DONE]`), so a ＋NEW clicked in the sliver between `[DONE]` and the socket closing cannot stamp a finished reply. **⚠️ PENDING FABLE QA:** (a) all three timeouts are BUILDER numbers (90s/75s/3s; 90 > the bridge's own 60s first-event error on purpose, so the bridge's clearer message wins whenever the relay is alive); (b) stop-then-proceed on ＋NEW / lane switch / session click rather than a "press Stop first" refusal — the brief preferred it, but it does mean a mis-click now ends a running turn (the feed says so); (c) `setMode` became `async` (all four call sites are fire-and-forget `onclick`s); (d) the hermes-lane-only watchdog asymmetry; (e) `turnHardRelease` duplicates three lines of the `finally`'s composer reset rather than sharing a helper — deliberate, because the `finally` also does holder-specific work (`expireApprovals`, `stampLiveTurn`, `convTurnEnd`) that must NOT run twice. **Files: `bridge/panel/index.html` (turn-lifecycle block + `sendChat` + `setMode` + the four session fns), `bridge/app.py` (`hermes_chat` heartbeat, ~L4225).** **Tests: new `bridge/tests/test_turn_lifecycle.js` — 43 checks** (force-end idempotence incl. the completed-turn rule, the abort, the hard release + its wrong-turn guard, Stop-armed-before-the-sid-guard by INDEX not just presence, the busy branch keying on the turn's lane, all four escape hatches asserted to reach `stopTurnNow` **and** to no longer contain `if (chatBusy) return;`, the lane-switch ordering before `chatMode = m`, the finally's timer+handle clear, the watchdog re-arm on every chunk, the constants read OUT of the panel and range-checked against the bridge's own guards, the bridge heartbeat + its position before the approval-pending `continue`, the panel's silent consumption of it, and four negatives pinning that the 600s guard, the 60s guard and the fail-safe probe were NOT touched). **All 23 py + 15 js suites green, contract 64, CSS 500/500 with ZERO new rules, ast OK, both panel JS blocks `node --check` clean.** **HONEST LIMIT: the wedge itself cannot be reproduced in the sandbox** (it needs a real hung gateway), so the fix is reasoned + wiring-tested, not run-verified; and the fix does not make the *turn* complete — it makes the *panel* recoverable, which is the part we own. **Debi verify:** `./scripts/ship.sh` → Hermes lane → send something heavy → while it is thinking, click **Stop** → within ~3s the reply stamps `interrupted (forced) · <stage>`, the button returns to **Send**, and the feed logs `turn force-ended at <stage>`. Then start another heavy turn and, WITHOUT stopping it, click **＋ New** → the turn is stopped and a new chat opens; repeat and click **AGENT** → the turn is stopped and the lane switches. Finally check a normal turn still completes cleanly (no `interrupted` stamp, no feed line).

The `CLI_PARITY_KWARGS` re-derivation rule is discharged **by measurement, not assumption** (tts/generate.py + stt/generate.py md5-identical across the two releases, `omnivoice/` identical), and that fact is now recorded in the yaml so the next bumper knows the rule still applies to THEM. ⚠️ **The honest risk is AUDIO, not API** — 0.4.8 is a resampling/istftnet release and our reference-clip path is exactly what it touches; and **`RenderCache` keys do not include the engine version, so 0.4.7-era renders keep being served after the bump** (A/B with fresh text or after a bridge restart). **PASS 2 (Hermes v2026.7.30 → v2026.8.13): the pin is bumped in harness.yaml and the ONE oversensitive contract assert is FIXED — in the TEST, per the sweep.** `test_hermes_ws_contract.py` pinned the literal `"from tools.approval import request_tool_approval"`; at v2026.8.13 that import is reformatted to a parenthesised multi-line block (plugins.py:5884) while `resolve_pre_tool_block` (L5851) still CALLS `request_tool_approval` (L5900). The replacement is **strictly stronger**: a regex accepting BOTH import forms **plus a new assertion that the call actually appears inside `resolve_pre_tool_block`'s body** — the original only ever pinned an import line, not the call site that opens the approval card. **Verified to pass at BOTH tags** (run against the v2026.8.13 blob read straight out of the fetched submodule objects, and against the currently-checked-out v2026.7.30). ⚠️ **NEW OPS GAP FOUND WHILE SCOPING THE SNAPSHOT SIDE, and it is the reason this pass needs a runbook rather than a script: `ship.sh` deliberately never copies `vendor/`, and the FAT snapshot carries its OWN `vendor/hermes` SOURCE COPY (staged by `build_app.sh --fat`'s rsync) with `.git` STRIPPED — so `install_component.sh hermes` run from the snapshot fails its own `vendor/hermes/.git` precheck, and a vendored pin-bump has NO sanctioned path into the fat app short of a full `--fat` rebuild + re-provision.** The snapshot's hermes venv is an **editable** install of that source dir, so replacing the source in place is most of the bump; the runbook therefore gives an `rsync --delete` (repo → snapshot, `.git`/`node_modules` excluded, AFTER the repo-side web_dist build so the prebuilt SPA travels with it) followed by an **ONLINE** `python -m pip install -e "<snapshot>/vendor/hermes[all]"` — online because 2565 commits of dep churn will not resolve against the pinned wheelhouse with `--no-index`. ⚠️ **PENDING FABLE QA: that rsync-into-the-snapshot is a NEW procedure, not an existing one.** The recorded alternative is the heavy-but-sanctioned `./scripts/build_app.sh --fat` rebuild; both are in the runbook and the choice is Fable's. **PASS 3 (llama.cpp b10295 → b10427): `runner.llamacpp_pin` bumped**, with every flag the sweep verified (`--alias`, `--repeat-penalty`, `--spec-type` + `DRAFT_MTP`, `llama-tts -m/-mm/-p/-o`, `--tts-lang`, `--tts-speaker-file`, and `-mv` still ABSENT) recorded inline, plus the ⚠️ **new implicit config surface** (`common_params_apply_system_config()` now reads `/etc/llama.cpp/config.ini` and `${XDG_CONFIG_HOME:-~/.config}/llama.cpp/config.ini` **if they exist**, applied before env and CLI — a no-op on Debi's Mac today, but llama-server's behaviour is no longer determined by our argv alone). `install_llamacpp.sh`'s awk reader returns `b10427` against the edited file. ⚠️ **`install_llamacpp.sh` OVERWRITES `data/llamacpp` and keeps NO backup of its own** — the runbook's first line for this pass is the `cp -R` that IS the rollback. **⚠️ THE PIN-READING GOTCHA THAT SHAPES ALL THREE RUNBOOKS: `ship.sh`'s manifest merge is ADDITIVE-ONLY, so a pin whose key ALREADY EXISTS in the snapshot's harness.yaml is NEVER updated by shipping.** `install_mlx.sh` and `install_llamacpp.sh` read the pin from *their own* directory's harness.yaml, so running them from the snapshot after `ship.sh` would cheerfully reinstall the OLD versions and look like a successful bump. The runbook therefore edits the snapshot's harness.yaml pin lines with three line-scoped `sed`s BEFORE running either installer (install_mlx.sh also honours `MLX_VLM_PIN`/`MLX_AUDIO_PIN` env overrides, given as belt-and-braces; `install_llamacpp.sh` has no such override, so the sed is mandatory there). **TWO STANDING FIXES FROM THE SWEEP, BOTH LANDED:** **(a)** the Odysseus branch lie is corrected in BOTH places — `harness.yaml`'s pin now carries an explicit BRANCH FACT block (`dev@25c9e73`; `merge-base --is-ancestor 25c9e73 origin/main` → NO, `origin/dev` → YES; `origin/main` cf4e240 is a stale side branch sitting BEHIND us containing a change upstream later reverted) and CLAUDE.md's "contract check of main@25c9e73" is now "dev@25c9e73" with the same correction. **The pin itself was NOT touched.** **(b)** new `test_bridge_never_pages_history` in `test_odysseus_msg_contract.py` asserts the invariant **on the side we control**: every `_ody_req` call to `/api/history/{sid}` in `bridge/app.py` (there are four) carries no `limit`, `offset`, `params` or `?`, and the test fails loudly if it can no longer FIND such a call (so it cannot pass vacuously after a refactor). Written this way deliberately because on the CURRENT pin those upstream params do not exist yet; the docstring carries the ⚠️ **instruction to add the upstream half at the Odysseus bump** (pin that `limit is None` takes the metadata-preserving in-memory branch in `routes/history/history_routes.py`), because after upstream #5929 the never-page rule is no longer only about `metadata._db_id` — **the paged branch CAPS AT 100 ROWS**, so it is now also the only thing standing between us and a silently truncated transcript. **VALIDATION (all in-sandbox, proxy env cleared): 23 py suites + 14 js suites green, contract 64 passed (was 63 — the new history test), harness.yaml parses, all four pin-reading awk one-liners (`install_mlx.sh` ×4 keys, `install_llamacpp.sh`, `bootstrap.sh` pin_for/repo_for ×5 components, `install_component.sh`'s VS_PIN) return the right values against the edited file, `bash -n` clean on install_mlx.sh / install_llamacpp.sh / ship.sh.** **HONEST LIMITS: nothing was installed or run on the Mac** — no MLX wheel resolved, no llama.cpp asset downloaded (the release CDN is blocked from here; the asset's EXISTENCE is the sweep's verified fact, not this session's), no Hermes reinstall, no `web_dist` rebuild, and **the submodule checkout is Debi's first step, not a fait accompli**. The three passes are deliberately SEPARATE so a `[voice] render` regression or a dead Hermes lane has exactly one candidate cause. **Debi runbook: in the session report, in order, with a verify + a rollback per pass.**

> **⚖️ OMNIVOICE LICENCE HONESTY + BF16 PATH SHIPPED (2026-08-14, Opus-5 builder to the Fable spec; Mac-verify PENDING, ⚠️ PENDING FABLE QA):** the provenance research proved our own licence gate was being defeated by an UPSTREAM MISTAG — k2-fsa's README licenses the WEIGHTS **CC-BY-NC** ("due to constraints from its training data (e.g. Emilia)") while every `mlx-community/OmniVoice*` repo tags itself `apache-2.0`, which is the CODE licence. Our badge reads the tag, so the model that would have been DISABLED as non-commercial came through green — the same gate that (correctly) refuses Spark-TTS and Voxtral-TTS. **(1) A KNOWN-MISTAG OVERRIDE TABLE, consulted BEFORE the tag at BOTH call sites.** New module-level `LICENSE_OVERRIDES` in `bridge/app.py` keyed by a lowercase SUBSTRING of the repo id (one key covers the six mlx-community repos, theoracleguy's seventh, k2's own and any future quant — listing them individually would go stale on the next upload), plus PURE `license_override(repo)` (returns a COPY, so the table can never be mutated through a response) and PURE **`audio_license_row(repo, meta)`** — the ONE seam the search rows and the single-repo probe now share, so a mistag can no longer reach the UI through one path and not the other (test-pinned: `audio_license_badge(hf_license(` appears exactly ONCE in app.py, inside that seam). The OmniVoice record: pill reads **`cc-by-nc (weights)`**, badge **amber**, reason **"weights CC-BY-NC per upstream README; code Apache-2.0"**, `source_url` = the k2 README, cited in a code comment. ⚠️ **PENDING FABLE QA — THE DELIBERATE ASYMMETRY: Get stays ENABLED.** An unambiguous `cc-by-nc`-TAGGED row is still DISABLED; a documented-but-mistagged one is badged and left downloadable, because this is Debi's own machine, personal use is unaffected by CC-BY-NC, and the honest move is to SAY the weights term rather than hide the model behind a button we switched off — the same verdict shape already recorded for unknown licences and for OpenRAIL. Flipping it to `nc` would disable Get on the model the harness now RECOMMENDS. A test asserts no override may carry the `nc` badge, so turning one off has to be a deliberate edit, not a typo. **(2) OMNIVOICE BF16 IS NOW THE RECOMMENDED STARTER.** `mlx-community/OmniVoice-bfloat16` added FIRST in `VOICE_STARTERS` — **id and size CONFIRMED LIVE against the HF tree API this session** (model.safetensors 1,225,189,510 + audio_tokenizer 805,665,628 + tokenizer.json 11,423,986 = **2.04 GB**, not a recon guess), described plainly ("the model VoiceStudio runs, in Apple-silicon form; clones any voice from a 10s clip — see the clip picker", 646 languages, no named voices so pin a clip or use ⊕ get starter voices), licence line **"code Apache-2.0 · weights CC-BY-NC (upstream README)"**. ⚠️ **the two Qwen3-TTS starters are DEMOTED IN COPY, NOT REMOVED** (Debi's verdict is "bad voices", but the GGUF one is still the only option that needs no MLX at all, and deleting a working card is a bigger move than telling the truth on it): the GGUF card now says voice quality is well below OmniVoice and that llama.cpp has no voice parameter at all; the MLX card says this Base conversion declares NO named voices (its `…-CustomVoice-8bit` sibling declares nine) and is "an alternative, not a recommendation". A test pins that both repos are still present and that exactly two cards carry the demotion phrase. **(3) MIGRATION: NOTHING AUTOMATIC.** An installed model is a file Debi chose and a clip is PINNED to it, so swapping it behind her back would strand the pin. Instead the Audio DETAIL pane of a tts entry whose id contains "omnivoice" and whose `size_bytes` exceeds 3 GB gains ONE faint line: *"a bf16 build (~2 GB, same quality) is available in Get voice models — download it, set it as default, re-pin your clip, then Delete this one."* PURE `omnivoiceBf16Hint(a)` (constants read OUT of the panel by the test, so a value changed there cannot pass against a stale expectation). ⚠️ **PENDING FABLE QA: a HARDCODED HEURISTIC** — an id substring plus a size threshold, the first time the panel reasons about a named model family. It cannot mislead (both facts come off the entry itself; the note only points at a download that already exists in the starters) and it is test-pinned to touch neither `dlStart` nor `setVoiceDefault`, but it is a judgement call. It also self-suppresses on a bf16 id (redundant with the threshold, kept as belt-and-braces). **(4) THE REASON IS NOW VISIBLE ON BOTH SURFACES.** New PURE `audioLicNote(row)` — a REAL reason (an override correction, or "use-restricted licence") earns one gold line; a tag-derived "licence unknown" does NOT (the pill already says it) and an `nc` row does NOT (the disabled-Get line already says it). Wired into the SEARCH ROW and the PROBE CARD (which also prints the `source_url`), because a mistag warning on one screen only is worse than none. **ZERO new CSS rules — CSS 500/500 unchanged**, both new lines use existing inline-style idioms and the existing `md-imnote`. **Tests: `test_audio_search.py` +40** (all seven OmniVoice spellings incl. an uppercase one → amber + the exact reason + the exact pill text + the source url; non-listed repos fall through to the tag unchanged; an nc-TAGGED row still disabled; an untagged row still amber-but-offerable; substring/case/copy/junk totality; the override-record key set; the no-override-may-be-nc rule) and `test_audio_search.js` +14 (the note table + both render sites + the starter card's repo/fmt/size/licence/first-position + the Qwen demotion) and `test_audio_rows.js` +11 (the hint decision table incl. the exact-3-GB boundary, the bf16 self-suppression, a big non-OmniVoice model, the STT negative, junk, the render site, and the nothing-is-automatic negative). **ONE existing assertion updated HONESTLY:** `test_audio_search.py` pinned the literal string `audio_license_badge(hf_license(m))` at the search call site — the SEAM moved, the badge logic underneath it is byte-identical, and the replacement assertion is strictly stronger (it pins BOTH call sites plus the single-use rule). **All 23 py + 14 js suites green, contract 63, CSS 500/500, ast OK, both panel JS blocks `node --check` clean.** **HONEST LIMITS:** the override table is the only thing standing between us and an upstream tag, so it has to be MAINTAINED by hand — a future family with the same code-vs-weights split will come through green until someone adds a row; and the bf16 build's real audio has not been heard here (the claim "same quality" is a dtype argument from the research, not an ear test — bf16 vs fp32 on the same 612,577,280 weights). **Debi verify:** `./scripts/ship.sh` → Models → Audio → **Get voice models** → **OmniVoice bf16 · MLX — recommended** sits FIRST at ~2.04 GB with both licence terms on the card → Get → when it lands, set it as default TTS, re-pin your clip, ▶ speak. Then select your OLD 3.27 GB OmniVoice row → **the faint bf16 line appears** under the actions (and does NOT appear on the new bf16 row) → Delete the old one. Also: **SEARCH HF ⌄** → search "omnivoice" → every row shows the amber **`cc-by-nc (weights)`** pill with the gold line *"weights CC-BY-NC per upstream README; code Apache-2.0"*, **Get is still enabled**, and clicking a row shows the same line plus the k2 README URL — while a search for "spark" or "voxtral" still shows a RED pill with Get switched off.

> **✅ FABLE QA + OMNIVOICE PROVENANCE VERDICT (2026-08-14, Fable 5):** starter-fix slice APPROVED (the monotonic-floor bug walking an accent-ordered table, the cached-None poisoning, and the flac-skip miss are all real and the 13/13 simulation is convincing; trim-guard numbers 12/15/30s accepted). speakText emoji strip Fable-built + tested (+3 cases). **PROVENANCE (Debi asked; research at docs/research/2026-08-14-omnivoice-provenance.md):** k2-fsa/OmniVoice IS the original (next-gen-Kaldi org, 613M Qwen3-0.6B backbone, 646 languages); every mlx-community/OmniVoice* declares base_model k2-fsa/OmniVoice with the IDENTICAL 612,577,280 params — a FORMAT CONVERSION, not a fork; VoiceStudio runs the SAME checkpoint via its own PyTorch code (the "k2/onnx build" note in earlier memory was WRONG — corrected here); its "800+ voices" = a gallery of instruct STRINGS + community reference clips, and our MLX path supports `instruct` too, so those design phrases work verbatim here. **⚠️ LICENCE FINDING (Fable verdict: FIX THE CARD, personal use unaffected):** k2's README licenses the WEIGHTS CC-BY-NC (Emilia data) while mlx-community mislabels the repos apache-2.0 (the CODE licence) — our licence gate was defeated by an upstream mistag. QUEUED: OmniVoice card/probe honesty note ("weights CC-BY-NC per upstream README; code Apache-2.0") + an override table for known-mistagged repos. ALSO QUEUED: suggest bf16 variant (2.04GB, same quality) over the fp32 3.27GB Debi has.

> **🎙️ STARTER-VOICES "2 ADDED — 11 FAILED" ROOT-CAUSED + FIXED, + CLIP-LENGTH GUARD (2026-08-14, Opus-5 builder; Mac-verify PENDING, ⚠️ PENDING FABLE QA):** Debi's `⊕ get starter voices` landed **2 of 13** (us-m-1 p311, us-m-2 p334) and reported eleven as *"speaker not found in the dataset mirror."* **That message was FALSE, and the sandbox could prove it: the HF datasets-server was reached LIVE this session (via web_fetch) and the `sanchit-gandhi/vctk` mirror is complete and contiguous** — offset 0 = p225, 39181 = p275, 50000 = p288, 53500 = p295, 60000 = p305, 70000 = p323, 80000 = **p345**, 86500 = p374, 88155 = **s5** (`num_rows_total` 88156, `partial:false`). All thirteen primaries are present; **nothing was missing and no substitution was needed to fix this.** Two defects, both in the LOOKUP half, neither of them network flakiness. **BUG 1 — DETERMINISTIC, EXPLAINS NINE SLOTS: the walk carried a monotonic `lo` floor across a table that is NOT sorted by speaker id.** `STARTER_VOICES` is ordered by ACCENT SLOT (us-m-1 p311, us-m-2 p334, us-m-3 p345, us-f-1 **p294**, …) because that is the order the picker should read in; the fetch then passed each found offset as the `lo` floor of the NEXT binary search, on the reasoning that "the next speaker is never earlier than the last" — which is only TRUE in ascending speaker order. On the fourth slot the floor was already sitting at p345 (≈ row 80 000), so p294, p339, p232, p243, p287, p225, p276, p245 and p252 were each lower-bounded **only in the region above p345**, where they cannot be, and each returned "absent". **This is reproduced as a REGRESSION PIN in the test file: the old walk finds exactly 4 of 13 against a synthetic column in which all thirteen are present; the fixed walk finds 13.** **BUG 2 — EXPLAINS THE REMAINING TWO (p345, p376) AND WHY THE MESSAGE LIED: a probe that FAILED was indistinguishable from a speaker that was ABSENT, and the failure was CACHED.** `vctk_speaker_bounds` returned `None` both when lower_bound landed on a different speaker and when the injected probe could not answer; the endpoint's probe cache then stored that `None` **permanently**, so one 429 anywhere in a run of **~200 anonymous requests fired flat out** (13 searches × ~17 probes + 13 pages + 39 asset downloads) both killed its own slot and poisoned that offset for every later search. There was **no retry, no backoff and no pacing anywhere in the path** — and the research had said in its own words that this endpoint "rate-limits" and is "a convenience service, not a CDN contract". **BUG 3 (silent, machine-dependent): the re-run skip test only knew `<slot>-<spk>.wav`**, but the no-ffmpeg degrade writes `.flac` — so a Mac without ffmpeg re-downloaded every clip it already had on every click. **THE FIX (bridge/voice.py + bridge/app.py only; no vendor, no new endpoint, no new dependency, ZERO new CSS):** (1) new PURE **`starter_fetch_order()`** sorts the WALK by speaker id — **the table keeps its reading order**, so the picker is unchanged and the floor optimisation (which is what holds the run inside the rate limit) is kept and made sound; the floor now advances **only on a PRIMARY hit**, because an alternate can sit near the start of the dataset and would re-create the very bug being fixed. (2) new **`VctkProbeUnavailable`** splits the two outcomes: `vctk_speaker_bounds` still returns `None` for a genuine absence but RAISES when a probe cannot answer, failures are **never cached**, and the slot reports *"could not reach the dataset mirror: … — click again in a minute"* instead of a false absence. (3) `_vctk_rows` now returns **(rows, total, reason)** and retries **4 times with exponential backoff + jitter** (pure, injected-jitter `vctk_retry_delay`, unit-tested), **paces** itself between requests, treats **429 as retryable and any other 4xx as fatal** (a 404 will not become a 200), and the per-utterance asset downloads ride the same discipline. (4) **`vctk_total_rows`** reads `num_rows_total` off the live page instead of trusting the hardcoded 88156, and the count is **PRIMED from one page before the first search** — a synthetic run of this exact walk caught that a stale-high total sends the first probe past the end of the data, where the server returns a perfectly good 200 with an empty list, which is not a failure and therefore cannot be retried out of (handled by a `_PastEnd` signal that shrinks the bound and re-runs). (5) **the offset index is PERSISTED** to `data/voices/starter/starter_offsets.json` — the research said "build an offset index once and cache it" and the first build did not; a simulated re-run drops from **117 requests to 14**. (6) **`starter_present()`** checks BOTH suffixes, so a re-run really does fetch only what is missing. (7) a **240s wall-clock budget**; slots past it fail with *"ran out of time — click again to carry on"* rather than hanging the request. (8) the research §6.2's named **accent-slot alternates** (p345→p360, p339→p299/p306, p287→p256, p276→p267, p245→p364, p252→p262, p376→p248) are now in the table and tried **only on a genuine absence** — ⚠️ **a substitution CHANGES THE VOICE and keeps only the accent slot, so it is reported in the response and named in the activity feed, never swapped in silently.** Live evidence says this path should never fire. (9) **every failure reason is printed to the bridge log too**, so the next diagnosis does not depend on the panel having been open — the absence of the reasons is what made this bug undiagnosable from Debi's report alone. **VERIFIED BY SIMULATION (the async walk had never been RUN before): against a synthetic speaker-ordered mirror, a clean run lands 13/13 and EVERY offset is the speaker's genuine FIRST row; at a brutal 25%-per-request 429 rate 10/13 still land and all three failures name the true reason ("rate limited"), none claim absence; the persisted index cuts a re-run to an eighth of the requests.** **PART 2 — CLIP-LENGTH GUARD (Debi's 7.28 MB, ~30s+ mp3 that pinned fine and then churned for minutes).** Bytes were the wrong unit: the 15 MB cap let it through, and what costs time is DURATION — while **mlx-audio's OmniVoice only ever listens to the first ten seconds** (`ref_audio_max_duration_s=10`), so everything past that is render time bought for nothing (and a longer clip also makes the pin-time transcription far more expensive). New PURE `wav_duration_secs` (real RIFF header math: byte-rate × data chunk, with a file-size fallback for the 0-length data chunk a streaming encoder writes, and chunk-walking so a `LIST` before `data` is stepped over), `estimate_clip_secs` → `(secs, method)`, and `clip_length_verdict(secs, have_ffmpeg, method)` → **ok · trim · warn · refuse**. At `/api/voice/entry-ref`: **trim** makes a bounded ≤12s mono 24 kHz copy in `data/voices/trimmed/` via ffmpeg (resolved by the existing `ffmpeg_bin` explicit-path ladder) and **pins THAT**, leaving the original untouched; **refuse** (over 30s with no ffmpeg anywhere) is a 400 naming the limit, the reason and the way out; **warn** accepts and says so. `/api/voice/library/save` applies the same verdict but only REFUSES (and deletes the just-written file) — trimming a stored recording behind the user's back would be a worse surprise than a slow render. A trim **drops any ref_text supplied for the original** (it no longer describes the clip being pinned) and the trimmed dir is listed by `library_entries` so the pinned copy actually shows as a chip — otherwise a pin pointing at it would read as "nothing pinned". ⚠️ **`REF_CLIP_TRIM_SECS = 12`, `REF_CLIP_LONG_SECS = 15`, `REF_CLIP_MAX_SECS = 30` are builder numbers.** ⚠️ **NON-WAV DURATION IS A SIZE HEURISTIC** — only a wav states its own duration in a header we can read for free, and our provisioned ffmpeg ships no ffprobe; `NONWAV_EST_BYTES_PER_SEC = 40000` (≈320 kbps, the TOP of the mp3 range) makes the estimate read SHORT for anything encoded lower, so it errs toward accepting a real clip rather than refusing one, and it can only cause a false refusal on a machine with no ffmpeg at all (with ffmpeg an over-estimate costs nothing — `-t 12` on a shorter clip is just a copy). **PANEL (small):** every failed slot's REASON now goes to the activity feed (it did before, but the status line said only a count — now it carries the first reason too), substitutions are named with the slot they fill, the pin surfaces the bridge's trim/refusal note instead of silently pinning a different file, and the picker note gains one honest sentence: *"Long clips are trimmed to ~12s — the model only listens to the first ~10 seconds."* **⚠️ PENDING FABLE QA:** (a) sorting the WALK rather than the TABLE (keeps the picker's reading order; the alternative was reordering a user-visible list to satisfy an internal optimisation); (b) the alternates table exists but live evidence says it can never fire — kept as insurance against a mirror that changes, at the cost of a path that cannot be integration-tested; (c) the 240s budget is a builder number and a slow-but-working mirror could now be cut off mid-run (it is re-runnable, and the offset index means the second click is cheap); (d) `trimmed/` is a second library SUBDIR that is listed but, like `starter/`, is **not reachable through `library_target`** — so a derived copy has no delete path (deliberate: a delete button on a derived file would only strand the pin it exists to serve, but the library is now two levels deep in two places); (e) the save endpoint DELETES a refused upload rather than keeping it; (f) `_PastEnd` is a second, internal failure type alongside `VctkProbeUnavailable`; (g) one existing assertion in `test_audio_rows.js` was widened HONESTLY (the `setEntryRef`→`stopSpeaking` proximity window, from 900 to 1600 chars — the invariant is unchanged, only the distance). **HONEST LIMITS: the real network path still cannot be exercised end to end from the sandbox** (signed asset URLs expire and there is no route from the bash sandbox — the live probes above went through `web_fetch`, which cannot download the flac assets or run the endpoint); ffmpeg is not runnable here so no trim was actually produced; and the size heuristic has not been checked against Debi's real mp3. **Tests: `test_starter_voices.py` 180 checks** (the regression reproduced in BOTH directions, probe-failure-vs-absence as different types, the live-observed `s5` ordering pinned, alternates + junk table, `vctk_total_rows`, the backoff table, the offsets index incl. corrupt-degrades-to-no-cache, `starter_present` incl. the .flac degrade and a zero-byte leftover, the full wav-header duration table, the verdict table, the trim argv and containment) plus the widened `test_audio_rows.js` line. **All 23 py + 14 js suites green, contract 63, CSS 500/500 with ZERO new rules, ast OK, both panel JS blocks `node --check` clean.** **Debi verify:** `./scripts/ship.sh` → Models → Audio → a cloning model → the clip picker → **⊕ get starter voices** → **the eleven missing slots land** (the two you already have are skipped) and any that still fails **names its reason in the activity feed** — if it says *"could not reach the dataset mirror … click again in a minute"*, click again and it carries on from where it stopped. Then drop or pick that **long mp3** → the feed says *"that clip is about NNs long — pinning the first 12s…"*, a `…-12s` chip appears pinned, and **▶ speak renders in normal time**.

> **⫽ SPLIT-VIEW **v2** — FOCUS MODEL, ONE STRIP, PER-PANE ✕ + CHAT INPUT AUTO-GROW (2026-08-14, Opus-5 builder to the Fable v2 redesign; Mac-verify PENDING, ⚠️ PENDING FABLE QA):** v1's split *worked* (the divider fix landed) but Debi's Mac verdict was that the **control model** was confusing — the right pane felt **stuck on Odysseus** (it reopened onto the persisted right tab), the **mini strip felt unresponsive**, **"left wins" was invisible logic** that produced a placeholder out of nowhere, panes resized oddly, and **there was no way to close just one pane**. v2 replaces the model wholesale rather than patching it. **`app/main.swift` ONLY on the shell side; the panel change is a separate one-function slice.** **(1) THE MINI STRIP IS DELETED.** `rightSeg`/`rightBar`/`rightTabChanged` are gone; there is **exactly ONE tab strip** (`labels: tabTitles` now appears **once**, and the test that used to assert *two* strips now asserts *one* — the fact it pinned genuinely changed). **(2) FOCUS IS THE MODEL.** Exactly one pane is focused; it wears a **2px gold top strip** (`paneGold` = the panel's `#c9a44d`) and the unfocused pane wears a **clear strip of the same height**, so switching focus moves **zero pixels of content**. The existing `.leftMouseDown` monitor now hit-tests **both** panes (`setFocus(1)`/`setFocus(0)`), and a click on the tab strip or `⫽` is in **neither** pane, so focus stays where it was — which is precisely what makes strip routing predictable. **(3) THE STRIP ROUTES TO THE FOCUSED PANE, AND COLLISIONS SWAP.** `tabChanged` writes the focused pane's tab; if the OTHER pane already holds that tab the two panes **swap** (`if idx == currentTab { currentTab = rightTab }` / the mirror in the left branch). One webview, two panes, and a **visible, predictable** outcome — "left wins" is gone. `syncStrip()` keeps `seg.selectedSegment` mirroring `focusedTab()` (including when focus changes by CLICK), and setting `selectedSegment` programmatically does not fire the action, so it cannot recurse. **The placeholder ("Already open in the left pane.") survives ONLY as a safety net** for a state the swap rule means we should never be asked for. **(4) PER-PANE ✕.** Each pane carries a 16pt borderless `PaneCloseButton` at its host's top-right (inset 8), faint→cream on hover via a real `NSTrackingArea` (a plain NSButton has no hover state and this floats over page content). **Z-ORDER IS STRUCTURAL, not re-raised**: `buildPane` adds strip → host → ✕, and every webview is reparented **into the host**, so a reparent can never cover the button. ✕ closes THAT pane: split off, the **survivor's** tab becomes the one tab (`if p == 0 { currentTab = rightTab }`), focus lands on the survivor. **`⫽` OFF goes through the SAME `closePane(1)`**, so the two gestures cannot drift. **`⫽` ON always opens on `(currentTab + 1) % 5`** with focus staying LEFT — deliberately *not* the remembered right tab, because reopening onto the remembered tab is exactly what made the right pane feel stuck. **(5) STATE**: four keys now — `harness.split.on` / `.left` / `.right` / `.focus`. ⚠️ **the LEFT tab is restored only when the split was ON** (reopening the arrangement is what was asked for; a single-pane launch landing on a stopped Voicebox would be a worse first impression than today's Mission Control), and a defaults file claiming both panes hold the same tab is repaired at restore. **(6) RESIZE.** `positionDivider()`'s degenerate-only rule is untouched (a dragged/autosaved divider is still left alone). Holding priorities are now set on **BOTH** panes — left **250**, right **251** (⚠️ arbitrary-but-adjacent; any pair with left < right is equivalent) — so a window resize moves the LEFT edge and leaves the pane you just opened alone; the right one can only be set **after** `addArrangedSubview`, so it lives in `setSplit`. **"Odysseus resizes weirdly" root-caused to REPARENT THRASH, and fixed**: `attach()` already early-returns when a view is in the right host, but `applyPanes` was unconditionally `removeFromSuperview()`-ing the **DropOverlay** (and the placeholder) on *every* pass — a real reparent of a live view on every tab click. Both are now guarded, and the overlay is re-added **only** when its host changed or a webview landed on top of it (`t.subviews.last !== ov`). **(7) DIAGNOSTICS KEPT, v2 fields**: `[split] tab -> …`, `focus -> …`, `close pane …`, `toggle -> on …`, and `applyPanes left=… right=… focus=… borrows=… parkHidden=… panes W/W`. **(8) DRAG-A-TAB-ONTO-A-SIDE is NOT in v2 — QUEUED as a Fable-lane enhancement.** **CHAT SPLIT (two chat COLUMNS) remains PHASE-3-GATED on the Fable state-isolation spec and was not touched.** **PANEL (separate, small): CHAT INPUT AUTO-GROW.** `#chat-input` was pinned at `rows="2"`, so a dictated paragraph scrolled out of sight while you were still speaking it. New pure-ish **`growInput(el)`** measures the natural rows=2 height **once, lazily** (the chat view can be `display:none` at load → `offsetHeight` 0 → nothing is cached and the next call measures again), remembers the border delta (`box-sizing:border-box` means `style.height` includes the border and `scrollHeight` does not), and sets an explicit height up to **`GROW_MAX = 3`×** the base. Wired at `oninput` (which covers **paste** too), inside `appendTranscript` and `convSend` (a programmatic write grows exactly like typing), and after `sendChat` clears the box (**shrinks back**). **ONE CSS change: `overflow-y:auto` on `#chat-input`** so it scrolls internally past the cap — CSS balance is **500/500, unchanged, zero new rules**. `#chat-inputrow` is already `align-items:flex-end`, so the Send/talk/auto/conv buttons stay bottom-aligned and nothing jumps. **⚠️ PENDING FABLE QA:** (a) **single-pane mode shows NO gold strip** — with nothing to distinguish, a permanent accent bar reads as noise, but the spec only defined the two-pane case; (b) the ✕ buttons are **hidden when split is off** (a lone pane's ✕ has nothing to close); (c) `⫽` ON **always** steps to `(currentTab+1)%5`, which means an in-session close-then-reopen does not restore the right tab you had — it is the literal spec and it is the direct answer to "stuck on Odysseus", but it does make `.right` persistence matter only across launches; (d) the LEFT-tab restore is gated on `split.on` (see above); (e) focus is still a **coarse bounds hit-test on `.leftMouseDown`**, so a keyboard-only pane switch does not move it; (f) the swap makes the placeholder unreachable — it is kept (and test-pinned) as an honest fallback rather than deleted; (g) `hermesVisible()` now asks whether EITHER pane holds Hermes, which **fixes** v1's pessimistic left-strip-only staleness stamp, and the stamp also fires when closing a pane; (h) `PaneCloseButton` is a new NSButton subclass in the shell (small, but it is a new type). **HONEST LIMITS: still NO swiftc in the sandbox** — validated by a string-aware balance pass (**braces 223/223, parens 590/590, brackets 39/39, final depth 0, no negatives**) and careful reading; **`./scripts/ship.sh` IS the compile check**. Nothing about `NSSplitView` holding-priority behaviour, the ✕ hover tracking, or live-webview reparenting between panes is run-verified, and `growInput` cannot be exercised headlessly (it needs a real layout). **Tests: `test_audio_drop.js` Phase-1 greps rewritten HONESTLY for v2** (one strip not two; the mini strip asserted GONE; all four defaults keys; the swap rule in both directions; `focusedTab`/`syncStrip`; the two-pane hit-test; the gold colour + 2px strip; both ✕ handlers + `closePane`'s survivor rule; `⫽` OFF sharing the close path; `⫽` ON stepping +1; both holding priorities; the placeholder-as-safety-net; the no-thrash guards; the overlay following the pane HOST; ⌘R; the v2 slog fields) **plus 6 new composer auto-grow checks**. **All 23 py + 14 js suites green, contract 63, CSS 500/500, ast OK, both panel JS blocks `node --check` clean.** **Debi verify:** `./scripts/ship.sh` → click **⫽** → two panes, **gold line on the LEFT** (focused), right pane opens on the NEXT tab → click inside the RIGHT pane → **the gold line moves right and the main strip jumps to that pane's tab** → now pick a tab on the main strip → **the RIGHT pane changes, not the left** → pick the tab the LEFT pane is holding → **the two panes SWAP** (no placeholder, nothing disappears) → drag the divider (neither pane goes below ~420) and resize the window (the LEFT edge moves) → click the **✕ on the right pane** → back to one pane on the left's tab → ⫽ again, then **✕ on the LEFT pane** → one pane showing what the RIGHT pane had → relaunch → the split, both tabs and the focused pane come back. Then Chat → type/dictate a long message → **the box grows to about three rows and then scrolls**, and **snaps back to two rows after Send**.

> **🔧 ⫽ SPLIT-VIEW "DOES NOTHING" — ROOT-CAUSED + FIXED (2026-08-14, Opus-5 builder; Mac-verify PENDING, ⚠️ PENDING FABLE QA):** Debi Mac-verified this morning's split slice and hit a dead button — the shell compiled, relaunched, and rendered `⫽` at the right end of the tab strip, but **clicking it did nothing visible on any tab**, with no crash. **ROOT CAUSE (primary, `app/main.swift` old L656): `setSplit(true)` did `splitView.addArrangedSubview(rightPane)` and NEVER POSITIONED THE DIVIDER.** An `NSSplitView` only derives pane frames in `adjustSubviews` / on resize; a pane inserted at RUNTIME arrives at the frame it already had, and `rightPane` was built as a bare `NSView()` — frame `.zero`. So the right pane really did join the hierarchy, at **0pt wide**, with the divider flush against the window's right edge. That is a precise match for every symptom: no crash (the insertion is legal), the button state flips, `applyPanes()` runs, the webview is reparented — and the user sees an unchanged window. **AGGRAVATOR (old L408-409): the two `widthAnchor >= 420` constraints were REQUIRED and were activated at startup, on a `rightPane` that had no superview.** The instant it joined, a required minimum the split view's own frame engine knows nothing about had to be reconciled with "left + right + divider == splitView.width"; Auto Layout resolves that class of conflict by BREAKING a constraint (logged to console, invisible to Debi) rather than by resizing. They were belt-and-suspenders — `constrainMinCoordinate`/`constrainMaxCoordinate` already enforce 420 on the drag — so they were the wrong thing to make required. **RULED OUT BY READING, not guessed:** the button fires (`seg` is added to `tabBar` BEFORE `splitButton`, so the button is on top; `seg` is centred and the button is trailing `-12`, and at `window.minSize.width` 900 a five-segment control cannot reach it), and `park.isHidden` is set once at construction and **never mutated anywhere**, `applyPanes()` included — so parked webviews cannot be covering anything (a hidden view does not draw regardless of z-order). **THE FIX (all of it, `app/main.swift` only — no panel, no bridge, no vendor):** (1) new **`positionDivider()`** — `layoutSubtreeIfNeeded()`, then `setPosition(max(420, min(total-420, total/2)), ofDividerAt: 0)`, called from `setSplit(true)` **synchronously AND once more via `DispatchQueue.main.async`** (at LAUNCH restore, `setSplit` runs before the window is on screen, so `splitView.bounds.width` is still 0 and the sync pass correctly no-ops — the async pass is that second chance). It **only intervenes when a pane is DEGENERATE (`frame.width < 420`)**, so a divider restored from the autosave or dragged by the user is left exactly where it is — the fix and Debi's "position survives a relaunch" requirement do not fight. (2) the two width anchors are now **priority 750, not required** — the engine satisfies them when it can and quietly relaxes them when it cannot (a non-required constraint is optimised, not broken, so no console noise either), while the HARD 420 floor still holds in the two places that matter: the drag delegate and `positionDivider()`. (3) `splitView.setHoldingPriority(249, forSubviewAt: 0)` so on a WINDOW resize the LEFT pane gives way rather than the pane you just opened. (4) **Permanent diagnostics**: a tiny `slog()` (`NSLog("%@", … as NSString)` — NSString cast so the `%@` CVarArg is unambiguous) emits `[split] toggle -> …`, `[split] setSplit(…) rightAttached=… arranged=…`, `[split] panes W/W of TOTAL`, and `[split] applyPanes left=… right=… borrows=… parkHidden=… panes W/W`. One click on `⫽` now says exactly what fired and what widths came out, via `log stream --predicate 'process == "Harness"'` or by running the binary from a terminal. **⚠️ TAMIC DELIBERATELY NOT FLIPPED on the panes** — the brief offered it, and the answer from reading is NO: `translatesAutoresizingMaskIntoConstraints = false` on arranged subviews IS the documented autolayout mode `NSSplitViewController` itself uses, whereas `true` would derive the pane's autoresizing mask from its CURRENT frame, which for a runtime-inserted `NSView()` is `.zero` — strictly worse than the bug being fixed. Children inside each pane keep their own constraints either way. **⚠️ PENDING FABLE QA:** (a) demoting a min-width constraint from required to 750 (correct here — the split view is a frame-based container and the delegate is the real enforcer — but it does mean the layout engine MAY hand back a sub-420 pane in some resize corner rather than refusing); (b) `positionDivider()` re-centres only when degenerate, which means a genuinely-restored 300pt autosave (from before the delegate existed) WOULD be corrected to centre — judged right, but it is a decision; (c) the async second pass fires one runloop after every `setSplit(true)`, including toggles where the sync pass already succeeded (it then no-ops via the degenerate check, but it is an extra layout pass); (d) `slog` is unconditional NSLog, i.e. these lines are always written to the unified log — cheap and deliberately permanent, but it is new logging in the release path. **HONEST LIMIT: still NO swiftc in the sandbox** — validated by a string-aware balance pass (**braces 189/189, parens 518/518, brackets 36/36, final depth 0, no negatives**) and careful reading; `./scripts/ship.sh` IS the compile check. **All 23 py + 14 js suites green, contract 63, ast OK — nothing outside `app/main.swift` was touched.** **Debi verify:** `./scripts/ship.sh` (recompiles the shell) → click **⫽** → the window splits and **both panes are roughly half the width**, the right one with its own small strip. **If it STILL does nothing:** quit the app, run `/Applications/Harness.app/Contents/MacOS/Harness` from a terminal, click `⫽` ONCE, and paste the `[split]` lines — they name the failing stage outright.

> **⫽ SPLIT-SCREEN PANES + CROSS-APP VOICE DROP SHIPPED (2026-08-14, Opus-5 builder to `docs/handoff/FABLE-SPLITSCREEN-SPEC.md` Phases 1+2; Mac-verify PENDING, ⚠️ PENDING FABLE QA):** Debi's two asks — **any two app tabs side by side**, and **drag a voice file from another app straight into the harness's voice controls**. Phase 3 (split CHAT) is GATED and was NOT touched. **PHASE 1 — ONE TAB STRIP, ONE OR TWO PANES.** The window content is now an `NSSplitView` (vertical, `autosaveName "harness-split"`, delegate-enforced **min 420** per pane plus a matching `widthAnchor >= 420` on each — feasible by construction because `window.minSize.width` is 900 > 2x420 + divider). A `⫽` push-on/push-off button sits at the RIGHT END of the existing strip (tooltip "Split view"); `harness.split.on` + `harness.split.right` persist in UserDefaults. **EVERY WEBVIEW STILL EXISTS EXACTLY ONCE** — the five properties are untouched; a pane BORROWS one by reparenting, and the ONE rule is **LEFT WINS, ALWAYS**: `rightBorrows = splitOn && rightTab != leftIdx`, so if the right strip asks for the tab the left is showing — or the LEFT later moves onto the tab the right holds — the right pane falls back to the placeholder **"Already open in the left pane."** and the webview stays/returns to the left. No negotiation, no second instance. A webview nobody borrows is parked in a hidden full-size `park` view, which is byte-for-byte the old `isHidden = true` state with a different parent (so a re-borrow needs no relayout and the page never reloads). The right pane carries its own compact mini strip at its TOP, built from the SAME new top-level **`tabTitles`** array as the main strip (asserted by test: exactly two `labels: tabTitles` sites — the five strings can never drift). Lazy-load is unchanged and now shared: `ensureLoaded(idx)` loads on FIRST borrow **by either pane**, and the failed-load retry (`retryIfFailed`) fires for whichever pane just selected. **The DropOverlay tracks Mission Control's CURRENT pane** — it is removed and re-attached (last, so it stays above the webview it guards) to whichever pane holds tab 0, and simply is not in the hierarchy when the panel is in neither. `⌘R` reloads the **focused pane**: `focusedPane` is set by a tab interaction on either strip and by a coarse `NSEvent` local `.leftMouseDown` monitor hit-testing the right pane's bounds — deliberately NOT a first-responder chase through WKWebView's internals. **PHASE 2 — AN AUDIO FILE DROPPED ON THE PANEL IS A VOICE CLIP.** `DropWebView.performDragOperation` now branches on suffix: images keep TODAY'S PATH EXACTLY (same mimes, same 8 MB cap, same `harnessNativeDrop`); **wav/mp3/flac/m4a** (the bridge's own `REF_AUDIO_SUFFIXES`, test-asserted to match) up to **15 MB** (`REF_AUDIO_MAX_BYTES`, also test-asserted to match) go to a new `window.harnessNativeAudioDrop(name, dataURL)`; anything else keeps today's behaviour (a note, no action) with the message widened to mention audio. The panel handler decodes the base64 to a `Uint8Array` and POSTs the **RAW bytes to the EXISTING `/api/voice/library/save?name=<stem>&fmt=<ext>`** — **ZERO new endpoints, zero bridge change, zero new CSS** — then writes one activity-feed line (`clip <name> added to the voice library`, name `esc()`d because `feed()` interpolates HTML) and calls `loadVoiceLib(true)`, which refetches and re-renders, so an open clip picker grows the new chip. **NO AUTO-PIN — a drop is an IMPORT, not a choice about which voice a model speaks in** (test-pinned as a negative: the handler contains neither `setEntryRef` nor `entry-ref`). This closes the loop Debi wanted: VoiceStudio history → download (now works, per this morning's slice) → drag the file from Finder onto the harness → it is a pinnable voice. **TRUE webview-to-webview drag (no Finder hop) is recorded as a WATCH-ITEM and deliberately not attempted.** **Tests: new `test_audio_drop.js` (24 checks** — the handler's existence/endpoint/stem+fmt/raw-bytes/suffix-set, the suffix and size caps byte-matched against `bridge/voice.py`, the feed line and its escaping, the library refresh, BOTH no-auto-pin negatives, the image path asserted untouched, and the Phase-1 shell greps: one titles array feeding two strips, both UserDefaults keys, the autosave name, min 420 on both constrain* delegates, the left-wins expression, the placeholder string, the overlay following the panel's pane, and ⌘R keying off `focusedPane`**). **All 23 py + 14 js suites green, contract 63, CSS 500/500 with ZERO new rules, ast OK, both panel JS blocks node --check clean.** **NO swiftc in the sandbox** — validated by a string-aware balance pass (**braces 182/182, parens 494/494, brackets 35/35, final depth 0, no negatives**) and careful review; **`./scripts/ship.sh` IS the compile check** (main.swift is newer, so it recompiles the shell). **⚠️ PENDING FABLE QA:** (1) **split OFF is not literally byte-identical any more** — the content view is always the `NSSplitView` with ONE arranged subview rather than webviews pinned straight to the container; every webview's parent moved from `container` to a pane/park view. This is what "borrowing" requires and the alternative (two parallel layout regimes) is strictly more failure surface, but it IS a structural change to a working path; (2) the "Already open in the left pane." surface is a plain `NSView` + centred `NSTextField` in the same palette, NOT the `showUnreachable` mechanism — that one loads HTML *into a webview*, and the whole point of this state is that the webview is busy in the other pane; (3) **toggling split ON when the right tab equals the left steps the right pane to `(currentTab + 1) % 5`** rather than opening onto a placeholder (builder choice — a placeholder as a first impression is useless); (4) `focusedPane` is a bounds hit-test on `.leftMouseDown`, so a keyboard-only pane switch does not move it; (5) the Hermes staleness stamp is still keyed on the LEFT strip only — with split on, Hermes may remain visible in the right pane, making the stamp pessimistic (worst case: one extra reload, never a missed one); (6) `park` is a hidden sibling of the split view rather than zero-sized, so parked webviews keep a full-size layout exactly as the old hidden ones did (a little more layout work per resize, no reload); (7) the ⫽ button is a `texturedRounded` push-on/off `NSButton` on the tab strip rather than a sixth segment — a segment would have joined the selectOne tracking group and broken tab semantics. **HONEST LIMITS / UNVERIFIED until the Mac:** NOTHING in the Swift half is compile- or run-verified — in particular the `NSSplitView`-with-autolayout arranged-subview behaviour, the divider autosave restoring across launches, and whether reparenting a live `WKWebView` between panes is as seamless as expected (it should keep its page and process; the drag re-registration in `viewDidMoveToWindow` is already written for exactly this); and the audio drop cannot be exercised headlessly (it starts in AppKit and ends at a `fetch`). **Debi verify:** `./scripts/ship.sh` (recompiles the shell — if swiftc errors it fails loudly there) → click **⫽** at the right of the tab strip → the window splits and the right pane gets its own small strip → pick a DIFFERENT tab on each side (e.g. Mission Control + Hermes) and confirm **both panes browse independently**; drag the divider and confirm neither pane goes below ~420; now select the RIGHT pane's tab on the LEFT strip → the right pane says **"Already open in the left pane."** and the page keeps working on the left (left wins); click inside a pane then ⌘R → THAT pane reloads; click ⫽ again → back to one pane, everything as before, and the split state (and divider position) survives a relaunch. Then: **drag a .wav from Finder onto the Mission Control pane** → the activity feed says `clip <name> added to the voice library` and the clip appears in Models → Audio → a cloning model → the clip picker (as an UNPINNED chip); finally **drag an image onto the Chat composer** and confirm attach still works exactly as before.


> **🐦 PARAKEET STT + VAD TUNING + VCTK-13 STARTER VOICES SHIPPED (2026-08-14, Opus-5 builder to the accepted research `docs/research/2026-08-14-stt-and-starter-voices.md`; Mac-verify PENDING, ⚠️ PENDING FABLE QA):** three slices, all riding things we already own. **PART A — A SECOND, SELECTABLE STT ENGINE (`stt-mlx-audio`).** Debi's constraint was that BOTH engines stay selectable, and the registry gives that for free: a whisper row and a parakeet row are two ordinary entries and the default-STT picker chooses. `STT_FORMATS = ("stt-mlx", "stt-mlx-audio")`. **NOTHING NEW IS PINNED** — `mlx_audio.stt.generate` is ALREADY in `data/mlx-venv/bin` under the existing `build.mlx_audio_pin: 0.4.7` that the TTS half rides, and `mlx_audio/stt/models/parakeet/` ships with it (both verified by reading the installed venv, not the docs). **THE REAL REASON TO ADOPT IT IS CORRECTNESS, NOT SPEED:** whisper is a seq2seq decoder with a text prior and reliably HALLUCINATES phrases out of silence ("Thank you.", "Bye."); in `conv` mode a phantom transcript is **AUTO-SENT as a message**, and the empty-transcript skip — our only guard — is precisely the guard whisper defeats. Parakeet's RNNT/TDT decoder has no text prior and emits **nothing** on silence, converting "phantom message sent" into "empty transcript, skipped", which is what the design already assumes. Parakeet 0.6B is *larger* than whisper-base (2.51 GB vs 137 MB) and its steady-state inference is a wash or slightly worse — **it is not adopted for latency, and the report says so plainly.** The argv branch is one pure function: `mlx_audio.stt.generate --model <dir> --audio <f> --output-path <dir>/<stem> --format json`, where `--output-path` is a **PREFIX not a directory** (`save_as_json` writes `f"{output_path}.json"`) — which is the single fact that lets **`stt_read_output` and `stt_text_from_payload` stay BYTE-IDENTICAL for both engines** (Parakeet's payload has the same top-level `text` key). Fallback form is `-m mlx_audio.stt.generate` with the same flags. **THE CLASSIFIER WAS THE HARD PART, EXACTLY AS THE RESEARCH SAID.** A NeMo config shares NOTHING with whisper's ModelDimensions shape (none of the six required keys, usually no `model_type` at all), so a downloaded Parakeet would have silently VANISHED from the Audio tab. New pure `is_parakeet_config` fingerprints the real thing: NeMo serialises its module graph with hydra `_target_` strings, so a genuine checkpoint carries `preprocessor/encoder/decoder._target_ = "nemo.collections.asr.modules.…"` — requiring **TWO** such blocks is what separates `mlx-community/parakeet-tdt-0.6b-v3` (ours) from `nvidia/parakeet-tdt-0.6b-v3` (a **transformers** repo with a parakeet NAME — the whisper-medium incident, second edition, and it is test-pinned as a negative). ⚠️ **The whisper transformers VETO is deliberately NOT applied to the shape rule** — a NeMo config legitimately carries `d_model`/`architectures`-ish keys and the veto would condemn every real Parakeet; a positive `nemo.` fingerprint is stronger evidence than the absence of transformers keys. `AUDIO_STT_TOKENS` gains **"parakeet"** — a BIRD, not a generic speech word, so it cannot start eating chat models the way "audio"/"voice"/"speech" would; **which** STT engine a folder is stays a CONFIG decision. The constants are replicated into `bridge/app.py` (which does not import `scripts/`) and the byte-identical sync is test-asserted, same as the whisper pair. **REAL BUG CAUGHT WHILE BUILDING: `audio_entry_view`'s engine pill was `"mlx" if fmt.endswith("-mlx")`** — `stt-mlx-audio` does not end in `-mlx`, so a Parakeet row would have been labelled **llama.cpp** in the Audio tab. Starter card added with the HF-API-VERIFIED repo id `mlx-community/parakeet-tdt-0.6b-v3` (library mlx, 1.43M downloads, cc-by-4.0, 2.51 GB — id, licence, file tree and size all confirmed live, not guessed), CC BY attribution on the card. It already surfaces in SEARCH HF → Speech-to-text with no query change (`filter=mlx` + `pipeline_tag=automatic-speech-recognition`). **PART B — VAD TUNING (research-ranked, pre-approved).** (1) **The noise floor now carries across re-arms.** The old conv re-arm was a bare `vadInit(frameMs)`, which threw away TWO different things at once — and only one was load-bearing. Clearing the frame buffer and the speaking/run/silence counters is what makes resuming mid-utterance from before the feedback gate impossible; discarding the learned NOISE FLOOR was collateral, and it cost a fresh **~600 ms deaf warm-up after every single reply**, in a room the segmenter had spent the whole conversation learning. New pure **`vadRearm(st)`** resets the machine and carries `noise`+`thr`. The warm-up gate needed NO new flag: it is `noise.length < warm`, so a carried full window is automatically not-warming, and a carried SHORT window correctly just continues warming. (2) **`hangoverFor(fmt)`** — 800 ms for whisper (that number was never a latency choice; it was tuned around whisper's hallucination behaviour), **575 ms for `stt-mlx-audio`**. Read ONCE at capture time so switching the STT default mid-session cannot retune a live listener. ⚠️ 575 ms is research-ranked, **not measured — it needs an EAR TEST**; too short splits an utterance at a natural pause and in conv mode a split **SENDS TWO MESSAGES**. Test-pinned at ≥500 ms and unknown/junk formats degrade to the SAFE 800. Combined saving ≈ **825 ms per conversational turn**, entirely from our own constants. **PART C — VCTK-13 STARTER VOICES.** One button in the clip picker downloads thirteen accent-diverse reference clips (~3 MB) into `data/voices/starter/`, **each with its ground-truth transcript stored** — so pinning one sets `ref_text` with **NO transcription anywhere**, neither the pin-time whisper call nor the per-render whisper-large-v3-turbo load. New `POST /api/voice/library/starter`. Fetch follows the research's plan exactly: the canonical VCTK release is a 10.94 GB zip with no per-speaker path, so clips come from the HF datasets-server `/rows` endpoint against the `sanchit-gandhi/vctk` mirror; rows are speaker-ordered, so each speaker's first offset is found by **BINARY SEARCH** (pure, injected probe, ~7 probes per speaker with a shared cache — this is what keeps the run inside the rate limit); signed URLs EXPIRE so metadata and audio are fetched in one pass; `/filter?where=` is never called (it returned an empty body for this dataset in testing). Three consecutive **mic1** utterances per speaker are concatenated to ~10 s / 24 kHz mono via ffmpeg. ⚠️ **NO-FFMPEG DEGRADE:** if no ffmpeg resolves, the single longest utterance is kept as **.flac** (in `REF_AUDIO_SUFFIXES`, read by miniaudio with no external tool) — ~4 s instead of ~10 s, surfaced honestly in the panel, never a silent failure. **PARTIAL SUCCESS IS THE DESIGN**: each slot lands independently, failures are reported per-slot with a reason into the activity feed, and a re-run only fetches what is missing. **CONTAINMENT: starter clips are listed by `library_entries` but are NOT reachable through `library_target`** — that function is the DELETE endpoint's boundary and it stays basename-flat, so the starter set has no delete path at all (correct: it is one button from being re-fetched, and offering a ✕ the bridge would refuse is worse than offering none). They pin by **absolute path**, the mechanism the folder feature already established. **ATTRIBUTION IS RENDERED, not just stored** — a one-line CC BY credit is always visible under the button with the full block (authors, publisher, DOI, licence URL, *changes made*, non-endorsement) one click away, plus the AI-voice/impersonation notice. ⚠️ **ETHICS, not glossed: a CC BY licence gives the right to COPY a recording, not to impersonate the person in it.** Hard exclusions honoured (p315 — transcripts lost to a disk error, so no ground truth; p280 — accent "Unknown"). **TESTS: new `test_parakeet_stt.py` (79 checks** — the argv table both forms, the whisper path asserted UNCHANGED, `stt_bin_for`, the reader against a real Parakeet payload, the full config decision table in BOTH copies incl. the transformers-parakeet negative and the byte-identical sync, `audio_probe_verdict`, `audio_format_probed` strict vs lenient on real temp trees, and the wiring greps**) + new `test_starter_voices.py` (93 checks** — the 13-slot table and its exclusions, the binary-search decision table incl. not-present/probe-failed/logarithmic-not-a-scan, the row picker's mic1-only rule and its totality against eight shapes of surprise JSON, the ffmpeg argv, the listing/containment/transcript behaviour on a real temp tree incl. six traversal refusals, and every required element of the attribution**) + new contract `test_mlx_audio_stt_contract.py`** (skips cleanly in-sandbox; on the Mac runs `--help` and pins `--model`/`--audio`/`--output-path`/`--format`+json, the console-script name, the exact pin, and that the parakeet family still ships in the pinned package). **Three existing assertions updated HONESTLY:** `test_conv_mode.js`'s re-arm grep (the reset half is unchanged and still asserted; the floor-carry is the new half), `test_vad_segmenter.js` (+29 checks for `hangoverFor`/`vadInit(opts)`/`vadRearm`), and `test_audio_search.js`'s probe-summary label (the two STT engines now NAME themselves — "Speech-to-text · MLX" alone no longer says which). **All 23 py + 13 js suites green, contract 63, CSS 500/500 with ZERO new rules, ast OK, both panel JS blocks node --check clean.** **⚠️ PENDING FABLE QA:** (1) the transformers veto NOT applied to the parakeet shape rule (reasoned above, but it IS a deliberate asymmetry with the whisper lane); (2) `is_parakeet_config` also accepts a bare declared `model_type: "parakeet"` when no transformers keys are present — mlx-audio injects that string itself, so a checkpoint carrying it is *probably* a repackaging, and this is inference; (3) `AUDIO_STT_TOKENS` widened at all — the token lists are "the whole safety margin" and this is the first addition since they were written; (4) the lenient (`strict=False`, our own tree) branch now picks the engine BY NAME when a config is unreadable — mirroring what mlx-audio's own loader does, but it is a name guess in a place that previously had one fewer outcome; (5) **575 ms is un-eartested** and is the single most likely thing to need a nudge; (6) the floor-carry means a room that changes mid-conversation (a fan switching on) is learned more slowly than before — the rolling 2 s window still adapts, but the first window of a session is now the only one that gets a clean slate; (7) starter clips live in a SUBDIR and are therefore listed by `library_entries` but invisible to `library_target` — deliberate, but it does mean the library is no longer one flat directory; (8) the starter section is placed in the CLIP PICKER rather than beside GET VOICE MODELS (it is only meaningful for a cloning model, which is exactly when that picker renders); (9) `_HF_ANY` is a NEW httpx client with no base_url and `follow_redirects=True` — required because the signed asset URLs point at a CDN host, but it is a second outbound client; (10) the binary-search trampoline (a pure sync search re-run against a growing async-filled cache) is unusual — bounded at 40 rounds, but it is a pattern, not a primitive. **HONEST LIMITS / UNVERIFIED until the Mac:** the ENTIRE network half of PART C could not be exercised (no route to the datasets-server from the sandbox; signed URLs expire anyway) — it is code-reasoned against a LIVE probe of the endpoint's real response shape and fails loudly per clip; **whether `mlx_audio.stt.generate` honours the exit-0-on-failure invariant is UNVERIFIED** (different CLI, different package — so the invariant is carried DEFENSIVELY: an empty transcript is a failure whatever the exit code, which is correct in both worlds, and the code is printed in the error text); real Parakeet accuracy/latency on Debi's voice; whether the 575 ms hangover splits her sentences; and the accent labels in every speech corpus are SELF-REPORTED, so all thirteen picks still want an ear check. **NOT DONE (deliberately, gated on a measurement): the persistent STT worker (research R6).** The research shows the recorded Gate-2 conclusion does not follow from its own evidence — `run2 ≈ run1` is equally consistent with "cold start is irrelevant" and "there is a large CONSTANT overhead", because `import mlx` is CPU work the page cache cannot help. The corrected in-process measurement is in research §3.4 and is a five-minute test; **run it before building R6.** **Debi verify:** `./scripts/ship.sh` → Models → Audio → **Parakeet TDT 0.6B v3** appears in Get voice models → Get (~2.5 GB) → set it as the default STT → `auto` chip: speak, then stay silent for a while — **no phantom "Thank you." appears**; `conv` chip: after a reply, listening resumes **noticeably faster** (no half-second deaf gap) and closes a sentence sooner. Then Models → Audio → a cloning model (OmniVoice) → the clip picker → **⊕ get starter voices** → thirteen chips appear (`us-m-1-p311`, `uk-f-1-p225`, `other-2-p252`, …) with the VCTK credit under the button → pin one → ▶ speak → **the reply is read in that voice and the FIRST render is fast** (no whisper transcription of the reference clip, because the transcript shipped with it). Switch the STT default back to whisper-base and confirm it still works — both engines stay selectable.

> **⬇️ WEBVIEW DOWNLOADS + GUIDE/TOUR EXPLAINER + SHIP LOCK-CLEAR SHIPPED (2026-08-14, Opus-5 builder to the Fable spec; Mac-verify PENDING, ⚠️ PENDING FABLE QA):** Debi hit it in the VoiceStudio tab — **clicking a download button did nothing at all.** Same silent-no-op class as `runOpenPanelWith` (which broke ⊕ attach) and `requestMediaCapturePermissionFor` (which broke ● talk): **a WKWebView saves NOTHING unless the host app answers the download delegate** — a response it cannot render and every `<a download>` click are simply DROPPED. FIX in `app/main.swift`: new `@available(macOS 11.3, *) final class DownloadHandler: NSObject, WKDownloadDelegate` — ONE instance **retained by AppDelegate** (`WKDownload.delegate` is WEAK, so a per-download handler would deallocate before `decideDestination` is ever called; the property is typed `AnyObject?` so the stored property itself carries no availability requirement). Destination = `~/Downloads/<suggestedFilename>` with **never-clobber " (n)" suffixing** (the artifact-save discipline — a generated clip or export cannot be re-made if we silently overwrite it) and **BASENAME ONLY** (`suggestedFilename` is page-controlled data and must never climb out of ~/Downloads; empty/`.`/`..` → `download`). `downloadDidFinish` carries no URL, so the handler keeps a `[ObjectIdentifier: URL]` map, cleared on finish AND on failure. Routing rides the SHARED navigationDelegate, so **all five tabs get it at once** (panel, Odysseus, Hermes, VoiceStudio, Voicebox): `decidePolicyFor navigationResponse` → `.download` when `!canShowMIMEType` (attachment disposition / unknown MIME), else `.allow`; `decidePolicyFor navigationAction` → `.download` when `shouldPerformDownload` (that is what WebKit sets for `<a download>`, **including the blob: URLs a SPA builds client-side**), else `.allow` — both are byte-compatible with the previous no-delegate default (`.allow`). `createWebViewWith` now skips `blob:`/`data:`/`javascript:` before handing a URL to `NSWorkspace.open` (handing it a blob does nothing useful); every other scheme behaves exactly as before. **Completion signal = `NSWorkspace.activateFileViewerSelecting` ONCE (reveal in Finder)** — deliberately not a notification (needs a bundle surface + something to dismiss) and not a page-side toast (the SPA is a third party we do not script). **⚠️ PENDING FABLE QA:** (1) reveal-in-Finder-on-finish as the only feedback — quiet and native, but it does steal focus to Finder once per download; (2) adding a `decidePolicyFor navigationAction` handler where there was none (returns `.allow` in every case but a download-attribute click, so no behaviour changes — but it IS a new decision point on every navigation in every tab); (3) the blob/data/javascript skip in `createWebViewWith` is a small behaviour change to an existing path; (4) `n < 1000` cap on the never-clobber loop (past that it overwrites rather than looping forever); (5) NO download progress UI and no cancel — WebKit does the transfer, the user sees the file appear. **HONEST LIMIT: a JS-initiated blob download only works if WebKit routes it through `shouldPerformDownload`** (macOS 11.3+ does for a real `<a download>` click); a SPA that instead opens a blob in a new window or uses a nonstandard save path would still no-op, and that cannot be tested from the sandbox. **PART 2 — the CHAT-mode explainer is now DOCUMENTED (Debi directive: it stays):** `docs/USER-GUIDE.md` gains a **"The note under the chips"** subsection (what *direct to runner — no tools, fastest* actually means: Chat is a straight line to the loaded model, Agent goes the long way round through Odysseus, Hermes the long way round through the agent — slower modes are slower because they do more) plus a **"Speaking and listening"** subsection (▶ speak / ● talk / auto / conv, one line each, incl. that **conv only appears when BOTH a TTS and an STT default exist** and that the macOS mic indicator going out is the honest release signal); `docs/USER-GUIDE-OPUS5.md` gains the same facts in ITS voice (a paragraph under §3 Chat + a new **§3 Voice** table with the `● listening / ● … / ▸ speaking` labels and the no-barge-in note). The panel **first-run tour's Chat step** now names the three chips and quotes the explainer — one step, no new step and **zero new CSS**. **PART 3 (ops):** `scripts/ship.sh` now does a best-effort `rm -f "$ROOT/.git/HEAD.lock" "$ROOT/.git/index.lock"` before anything else — the sandbox cannot unlink files under the mount, so an interrupted git op there leaves locks that fail every later commit with "File exists"; ship.sh runs on the Mac, where they ARE removable. Failure is swallowed. **⚠️ PENDING FABLE QA — ops-path edit (ship.sh is Fable-lane), though safe by construction: no git process runs during ship.sh.** **NO Swift compile possible in the sandbox** — validated by a string-aware balance pass (braces 150/150, parens 412/412, brackets 26/26, final depth 0, no negatives) and careful reading; **Debi's `./scripts/ship.sh` IS the compile check** (it recompiles the shell because main.swift is newer). **All 21 py + 13 js suites green, contract 57, CSS 500/500, ast OK, both panel JS blocks node --check clean.** **Debi verify:** `./scripts/ship.sh` (recompiles the shell — if swiftc errors, it fails loudly there) → VoiceStudio tab → click a download button on a history item → **the file lands in ~/Downloads and Finder opens revealing it**; a second download of the same name becomes `name (2).ext`. Also sanity-check the other tabs still load and target=_blank links still open in the browser.

> **✅ FABLE QA: CONVERSATION MODE APPROVED (2026-08-14, Fable 5; Debi-verified working on the Mac):** all 5 builder tags approved — barge-in out of scope was the spec; state-in-the-chip fine; shared capture with a mode field = one teardown path (right); the segmenter reset + fresh warm-up per re-arm trades ~600ms latency for making self-transcription structurally impossible (correct trade, latency noted for the faster-STT research); typed-mid-conv spoken back + composer prepend approved. Suites independently re-run green. The feedback-gate table (fail-closed, one ungated phase) is the standout of the slice.

> **🗣️ VOICE CONVERSATION MODE SHIPPED — HANDS-FREE `conv` CHIP (2026-08-14, Opus-5 builder to the Fable spec; Mac-verify PENDING, ⚠️ PENDING FABLE QA):** Debi's ask, exactly: **speak → the utterance AUTO-SENDS → the reply streams → the reply is AUTO-SPOKEN → listening resumes.** A third composer chip **`conv`** sits AFTER `auto` (same 7px sizing rule — the selector was widened, not duplicated) and is visible only when **BOTH an STT and a TTS default exist** (`convAvail() = sttOn() && voiceOn()` — either alone is useless, and a chip that only half-works is worse than no chip). Panel-only slice: **no new endpoint, no bridge change, no new dependency, ZERO new CSS rules** (three selector extensions: the hover rule, the size rule, and the gold `.talk-dot` rule now name `#chat-conv` alongside `#chat-auto`). **THE CAPTURE HALF IS SHARED, NOT DUPLICATED:** `startAuto(mode)` gained a mode ('auto' | 'conv') and the session object carries it, so there is still exactly ONE worklet, ONE segmenter and ONE mic teardown (`stopAuto`) — a second copy of the VAD machinery would have been a second way to leave the mic light on. `autoOn()`/`convOn()` now each name WHICH mode holds the session, so the two chips can never both read as live. **THE FEEDBACK-LOOP GATE IS THE WHOLE FEATURE.** A loop with a live mic at one end and a loudspeaker at the other is self-sustaining if it is wrong in any single phase: the harness hears itself, transcribes itself, answers itself, forever. So the VAD is gated OFF in **every phase but LISTENING** — including THINKING (an STT call in flight), WAITING (the reply is streaming) and SPEAKING (the reply is playing out loud) — plus a **300ms grace** after playback before the segmenter re-arms, because a clip's tail and the speaker's own decay would otherwise open an utterance made of our own voice. The gate is applied in `autoFrame` **before the block is buffered or measured**, so nothing the assistant says can be segmented, transcribed, or taught to the noise floor. The mic STREAM stays open (re-acquiring it every turn would re-prompt and flicker the OS indicator); it is the SEGMENTER that is suspended. On re-arm the segmenter is **RESET** (`vadInit`, frames cleared) so it can never resume mid-utterance from before the gate, and each listening window re-learns the room. **THE STATE MACHINE IS A NAMED PURE OBJECT, NOT SCATTERED FLAGS:** `convInit` / `convStep(st, ev, detail) → {st, action}` / `convGated(st)` / `convLabel(st)` are PURE (a NEW state object per event — asserted by test — no DOM, no audio, no fetch); `convEvent()` is the ONLY place an action becomes a side effect.
